# General forms task witnesses

Snapshot `9a9d110af839b539fe598de66ecc4320a4126af4`. Source order and source IDs are authoritative. All assigned sections and their examples, hints, graphics source, tables, and code were semantically read before old dispositions were consulted. No textbook edits.

Each task row includes an exact source identity, a solution witness, its support IDs, and a verdict. The linked witness is part of the row: it supplies actual formulas, sign checks, proof outline, or a complete permitted project choice. Report-deliverable rows inherit the same mathematical witness. Alternative exploratory datasets/software runs are not fabricated as executed; they do not require a universal solution. All high-risk listed options (cone, entropy, fold, closed/exact alternatives) are checked explicitly.

Inventory contains 258 assigned candidates. There are 710 recorded task identities after adding source list items and prompt paragraphs; overlapping inventory paragraph and containing list-item identities are retained as aliases, not counted as different exercises. 8 inventory candidates are explicitly excluded below.

## Solution witnesses

### checkpoint-16-1-why-orient-points

Opposite endpoint signs produce f(b)-f(a); all-positive points would produce a sum and would not telescope.

Support: `c16s1-subsec-0-forms-functions` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-what-is-a-differential-form.xml:108–137).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-1-why-wedge-sign

Swapping the ordered tangent edges reverses signed area, while ordinary area forgets that order.

Support: `c2s7-def-wedge-product-dx-dy` (source/chapters/ch02-vector-products/sections/sec-2-alternating-products-a-first-glimpse-of-forms.xml:277–289).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-1-why-stop-at-three

Four tangent inputs in R3 are linearly dependent; an alternating four-direction measurement is zero. The coordinate construction has only three distinct directions.

Support: `c2s7-def-volume-form-dx-dy-dz` (source/chapters/ch02-vector-products/sections/sec-2-alternating-products-a-first-glimpse-of-forms.xml:434–446).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-2-vector-vs-covector

A vector is a displacement input; a covector is a linear scalar-valued measurement of that input. Coordinates alone do not identify their roles.

Support: `c16s2-def-covector` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-covectors-and-differentials.xml:53–69).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-2-covector-field-meaning

The base point selects the local measuring coefficients: e.g. wind/work depends on where the same velocity occurs.

Support: `c16s2-def-evaluating-a-1-form` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-covectors-and-differentials.xml:246–259).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-2-why-gradient-needs-dot-product

The inner product represents the linear functional by a vector; df exists without that metric choice. Different metrics change its representing gradient.

Support: `c16s2-thm-differential-represented-by-gradient` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-covectors-and-differentials.xml:355–376).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-3-sign-means-orientation

An ordered pair of edges determines orientation. Reversing that order negates the determinant and signed area.

Support: `c16s3-def-wedge-product-two-covectors` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-the-wedge-product.xml:141–160).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-3-why-self-wedge-vanishes

Two identical scalar measurements cannot distinguish two independent directed area coordinates: alpha(u)alpha(v)-alpha(v)alpha(u)=0.

Support: `c16s3-def-wedge-product-two-covectors` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-the-wedge-product.xml:141–160).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-3-why-three-shadows-in-space

There are three distinct coordinate two-direction choices xy,yz,zx in R3, but just xy in R2; each gives a signed projected area.

Support: `c16s3-ex-tilted-surface-patch` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-the-wedge-product.xml:459–486).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-4-why-degrees-do-not-mix

Homogeneous k-forms take k tangent inputs. A one-input and two-input measurement are not a single homogeneous form; the text is not claiming mixed-degree sums are impossible in a larger algebra.

Support: `c16s1-def-1-form` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-what-is-a-differential-form.xml:230–241); `c16s1-def-2-form` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-what-is-a-differential-form.xml:337–348).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-4-no-room-above-n

Expand a coordinate (n+1)-fold wedge: one coordinate factor repeats, hence alternation makes each term zero. The checkpoint itself supplies this hint.

Support: `c16s4-def-multiply-form-by-function` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-algebra-of-forms.xml:149–157); `checkpoint-16-4-no-room-above-n` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-algebra-of-forms.xml:193–209).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-4-sign-as-orientation

Moving k ordered directions past l others entails kl adjacent swaps, each reversing orientation; total sign is (-1)^(kl).

Support: `c16s4-thm-sign-rule-swapping` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-algebra-of-forms.xml:295–316).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-5-why-pullback-back

First push tangent vectors by DT, then apply the target measurement. This defines a source covector, so forms travel from target to source.

Support: `c16s5-def-pullback-coordinate` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-pullbacks.xml:92–107).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-5-wedge-becomes-cross

The three coordinate two-by-two minors are exactly the components of r_u cross r_v: both encode signed projected areas.

Support: `c16s5-def-pullback-coordinate` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-pullbacks.xml:92–107); `c16s3-ex-tilted-surface-patch` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-the-wedge-product.xml:459–486).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-5-jacobian-sign

A negative determinant means the parameter coordinate frame induces the opposite target orientation. Absolute area removes that sign; an oriented form retains it.

Support: `c16s5-def-pullback-coordinate` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-pullbacks.xml:92–107); `c12s5-subsec-orientation-versus-absolute-value` (source/chapters/ch12-change-of-variables/sections/sec-12-pullbacks-of-area-and-volume-elements.xml:372–459).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-6-degree-matches-curve

Pulling back a 1-form produces one coefficient times dt, measuring one tangent velocity. A two-form pulls back to zero on a one-dimensional parameter interval.

Support: `c13s3-def-work-integral` (source/chapters/ch13-vector-fields-line-integrals/sections/sec-13-work-integrals-of-vector-fields.xml:167–178); `c16s5-ex-form-pulled-back-parabola` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-pullbacks.xml:231–251).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-6-why-independence-holds

A compatible once-covering reparametrization changes the coefficient and the parameter increment by inverse compensating substitution factors. Curve substitution and surface change of variables provide the actual operation.

Support: `c16s6-def-integral-1-form-curve` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-integrating-forms.xml:58–69); `c16s6-def-integral-2-form-surface` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-integrating-forms.xml:150–164); `c12s3-thm-change-of-variables-plane` (source/chapters/ch12-change-of-variables/sections/sec-12-nonlinear-changes-of-variables-in-the-plane.xml:152–196).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-16-6-orientation-gives-sign

Changing a compatible oriented frame to the opposite one changes the evaluation of an alternating form by -1; ordinary unsigned area is a different integral.

Support: `c16s6-thm-reparametrize-curve` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-integrating-forms.xml:318–346); `c16s6-thm-reparametrize-surface` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-integrating-forms.xml:409–425).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-17-1-measures-not-stores

dT_P is the whole linear map (a,b) maps to 3a-b/3; 7 is only its evaluation at (2,-3).

Support: `c16s2-def-covector` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-covectors-and-differentials.xml:53–69).

df is already a scalar-valued linear measurement; the dot product identifies its representing gradient vector.

### checkpoint-17-1-speed-scales-rate

Linearity gives df(2v)=2df(v): same heading at twice the speed changes the reading twice as fast.

Support: `c17s1-def-exterior-derivative-0-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-0-form.xml:179–194).

### checkpoint-17-2-boundary-meets-inside

Add local circulation balances; each internal edge occurs in both directions, leaving the outer boundary.

Support: `c14s4-thm-greens-for-1-forms` (source/chapters/ch14-greens-theorem/sections/sec-14-green-s-theorem-in-differential-form-language.xml:201–217).

Equal mixed coefficients cancel because dy wedge dx=-dx wedge dy and repeated directions vanish. A symmetric product would add them.

For sufficiently regular forms, Stokes applied twice relates integral of d² eta to the empty twice-boundary, both zero. This is a consistency interpretation, not an independent proof of arbitrary patching.

### checkpoint-17-5-why-exact-is-closed

A global primitive gives cancellation of mixed partials everywhere and zero relevant boundary integrals. Closedness only tests local coefficients and need not provide that primitive.

Support: `c17s5-def-exact-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-closed-and-exact-forms.xml:44–64); `c17s5-def-closed-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-closed-and-exact-forms.xml:152–160); `c17s4-thm-d-domega-zero` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-rule-d-squared-equals-zero.xml:283–309).

### checkpoint-17-5-what-the-hole-breaks

The origin prevents a global single-valued angle. The unit-circle integral is 2pi, which rules out a global potential although the local coefficient derivative is zero.

### checkpoint-19-4-surface-independence

### checkpoint-18-2-why-d-raises-degree

d converts the boundary measurement into its interior k-dimensional density.

Support: `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-2-why-interior-cancels

Adjacent regions traverse a shared edge oppositely and contribute equal negative integrals.

Support: `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-3-why-minus-sign

The positively traversed interval enters at a and exits at b, hence signed boundary b-a.

Support: `c18s3-thm-ftc-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-fundamental-theorem-of-calculus-as-stokes.xml:100–111).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-3-interior-cancellation

Interior partition points are an exit of one interval and entry of the next; their function values cancel.

Support: `c18s3-thm-ftc-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-fundamental-theorem-of-calculus-as-stokes.xml:100–111).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-3-boundary-theorem-view

The total derivative integral is endpoint data f(b)-f(a), an oriented boundary measurement.

Support: `c18s3-thm-ftc-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-fundamental-theorem-of-calculus-as-stokes.xml:100–111).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-4-curl-meaning

Q_x-P_y is infinitesimal counterclockwise circulation per unit signed area.

Support: `c17s2-def-exterior-derivative-plane-1-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-1-form.xml:142–153); `c14s2-thm-green-circulation` (source/chapters/ch14-greens-theorem/sections/sec-14-green-s-theorem-circulation-form.xml:86–104).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-4-inner-orientation

Changing the inner direction adds its area instead of subtracting the hole, breaking Stokes.

Support: `c18s4-thm-green-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-green-s-theorem-as-stokes.xml:183–202); `c18s1-def-positive-boundary-orientation-plane` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:213–226).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-4-one-theorem

Use either the work form P dx+Q dy or the flux form P dy-Q dx; d computes curl or divergence accordingly.

Support: `c18s4-thm-green-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-green-s-theorem-as-stokes.xml:183–202); `c18s1-def-positive-boundary-orientation-plane` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:213–226).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-5-why-curl-appears

Expanding d(P dx+Q dy+R dz) with antisymmetry produces exactly the three curl coefficients.

Support: `c17s2-thm-exterior-derivative-work-form-is-curl` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-1-form.xml:286–308).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-5-only-the-boundary

For this exact 2-form d omega, all admissible surfaces induce the same boundary integral; arbitrary 2-forms need not.

Support: `c17s2-thm-exterior-derivative-work-form-is-curl` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-1-form.xml:286–308); `c18s5-thm-classical-stokes-as-generalized` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-classical-stokes-theorem-as-generalized-stokes.xml:286–306).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-5-missing-point-circulation

The chosen spanning disk includes a point where the form is undefined, so its hypotheses fail.

Support: `c17s2-thm-exterior-derivative-work-form-is-curl` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-1-form.xml:286–308); `c18s5-thm-classical-stokes-as-generalized` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-classical-stokes-theorem-as-generalized-stokes.xml:286–306).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-6-form-degree-jump

Boundary flux is measured on a surface; total source density is measured in the enclosed volume.

Support: `c17s3-thm-d-flux-is-divergence-volume-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-2-form.xml:227–247).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-6-net-flux-meaning

Only net crossing remains after internal transfers cancel; the boundary integral equals total divergence.

Support: `c17s3-thm-d-flux-is-divergence-volume-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-2-form.xml:227–247); `c18s6-thm-divergence-theorem-as-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-divergence-theorem-as-generalized-stokes.xml:212–229); `c17s4-thm-d-domega-zero` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-rule-d-squared-equals-zero.xml:283–309).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-6-curl-flux-vanishes

d(d omega)=0 for C2 coefficients, so Stokes on the solid gives zero closed-surface flux.

Support: `c17s3-thm-d-flux-is-divergence-volume-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-2-form.xml:227–247); `c18s6-thm-divergence-theorem-as-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-divergence-theorem-as-generalized-stokes.xml:212–229); `c17s4-thm-d-domega-zero` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-rule-d-squared-equals-zero.xml:283–309).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-7-why-interior-edges-cancel

Each shared edge carries opposite induced orientations, so its two integrals cancel.

Support: `c18s3-thm-ftc-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-fundamental-theorem-of-calculus-as-stokes.xml:100–111); `c10s3-thm-fubini-rectangles` (source/chapters/ch10-double-integrals/sections/sec-10-iterated-integrals-and-fubini-s-theorem.xml:217–250); `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-7-why-pieces-assemble

Add exact rectangle identities and cancel shared edges; general curved-domain limiting is separately an outline.

Support: `c18s3-thm-ftc-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-fundamental-theorem-of-calculus-as-stokes.xml:100–111); `c10s3-thm-fubini-rectangles` (source/chapters/ch10-double-integrals/sections/sec-10-iterated-integrals-and-fubini-s-theorem.xml:217–250); `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-7-twin-facts

Apply Stokes twice at the appropriate degrees: the twice boundary cancels, and the integral of d² is zero.

Support: `c18s3-thm-ftc-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-fundamental-theorem-of-calculus-as-stokes.xml:100–111); `c10s3-thm-fubini-rectangles` (source/chapters/ch10-double-integrals/sections/sec-10-iterated-integrals-and-fubini-s-theorem.xml:217–250); `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-8-why-signs-on-points

Outward/end signs are b-a and recover the endpoint difference, not a sum.

Support: `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88); `c18s1-def-outward-first-convention` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:435–452).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-8-why-degree-two

The boundary of a solid is two-dimensional and measures two tangent directions.

Support: `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88); `c18s1-def-outward-first-convention` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:435–452).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-8-one-theorem-four-faces

Dimensions 1,2,2-in-space,3 and the work/flux dictionaries yield FTC, Green, classical Stokes, divergence.

Support: `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88); `c18s1-def-outward-first-convention` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:435–452).

Verdict: supported.

### checkpoint-18-9-orientation-truth-value

Only the clockwise line integral was negated; the fixed positively oriented square integral remained 1.

Support: `c18s4-thm-green-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-green-s-theorem-as-stokes.xml:183–202); `c14s6-a-jump-inside-is-a-hidden-edge` (source/chapters/ch14-greens-theorem/sections/sec-14-warning-examples.xml:286–342); `c17s2-ex-hidden-singularity-breaks-theorem` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-1-form.xml:455–497).

The field is defined only on a punctured domain; the disk needed by the careless step is inadmissible.

### c18s10-ex-pr-any-surface

Under the intended additional compactness assumption, 3 dx wedge dy=d(3x dy) and the induced CCW unit-circle integral gives3pi. As literally stated, the task is under-specified: the smooth upward exterior planar surface z=0,x²+y²>=1 has the same geometric boundary circle (induced clockwise) and integral3dxdy diverges. The compact Stokes theorem cannot apply. Add compact and state induced CCW orientation.

Support: `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88); `c18s1-def-outward-first-convention` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:435–452); `c16s6-def-integral-1-form-curve` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-integrating-forms.xml:58–69); `c16s6-def-integral-2-form-surface` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-integrating-forms.xml:150–164); `c18s4-thm-green-as-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-green-s-theorem-as-stokes.xml:183–202); `c18s6-thm-divergence-theorem-as-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-divergence-theorem-as-generalized-stokes.xml:212–229).

Verdict: GF-05: hypothesis gap; intended answer3pi conditional on compactness.

### c19s6-project-heat-plate

u_t=-2 alpha pi²u and Delta u=-2pi²u; each boundary sine vanishes and t=0 gives the required initial data. For alpha>0 the exponential decays. H(t)=4 exp(-2alpha pi²t)/pi². The (3,1) spatial mode has Delta eigenvalue -10pi² and exponential exp(-10alpha pi²t), five times faster decay. Larger curvature imbalance creates greater diffusive flux.

### c19s6-project-stream-functions

With C2 psi, div(psi_y,-psi_x)=psi_yx-psi_xy=0. For psi=sin x sin y, v=(sin x cos y,-cos x sin y). Its dot product with grad psi is identically0, so the chain rule makes psi constant along each differentiable trajectory. Scalar curl=-psi_xx-psi_yy=-Delta psi=2sin x sin y for the example.

### c19s6-project-maxwell

dE=(E3_y-E2_z)dy dz+(E1_z-E3_x)dz dx+(E2_x-E1_y)dx dy. dB=(B1_x+B2_y+B3_z)dx dy dz. Thus Faraday is curl E+B_t=0 and dB=0 is div B=0. Applying d gives (dB)_t=0 because d²E=0 and C2 space/time derivatives commute; this is redundant with the magnetic constraint, not an additional independent Maxwell equation.

### c19s6-project-harmonic

x,y,xy have zero xx and yy sum. Boundary values for x: bottom/top x, left0,right1; for y: bottom0,top1,left/right y; for xy: bottom/left0,top x,right y. For a x+b y+c xy, linearity gives Delta=0. Its source proof has GF-03 missing connectedness support; applying the correctly stated theorem is allowed.

### c20s1-proj-quadric-surfaces

A complete gallery can choose cylinder x²+y²=4 (z-slices circles, x/y-slices parallel vertical lines); parabolic cylinder y=x² (z-slices parabolas, x=c gives y=c² vertical line); bowl z=x²+y² (horizontal circles, coordinate planes parabolas); saddle z=x²-y² (horizontal hyperbolas, coordinate sections opposite parabolas); ellipsoid x²/9+y²/4+z²=1 (three central ellipses with semiaxes3,2,1); one-sheet x²+y²-z²=1 (horizontal circles radius sqrt(1+z²), central vertical hyperbolas). Two-sheet z²-x²-y²=1 has pieces z>=1,z<=-1; cone z²=x²+y² has two nappes joined at the vertex and is connected. Translated ellipsoid has center(1,-2,0), semiaxes(2,3,4). A missing variable means translation-invariant extrusion. Names/traces/hand sketches and the comparison questions require the geometric meaning of connected pieces, not the clopen criterion.

Support: `c20s1-proj-quadric-surfaces` (source/chapters/ch20-capstone-projects/sections/sec-20-geometry-and-visualization-projects.xml:20–79); `c4s4-ex-hyperboloid-one-sheet` (source/chapters/ch04-curves-coordinates-surfaces/sections/sec-4-quadric-surfaces-and-level-surfaces.xml:411–434); `c4s4-ex-hyperboloid-two-sheets` (source/chapters/ch04-curves-coordinates-surfaces/sections/sec-4-quadric-surfaces-and-level-surfaces.xml:436–458).

### c20s1-proj-gradient-fields

Choose f1=4-x²/4-y² and f2=xy. Gradients are(-x/2,-2y) and(y,x); contour tangent (dx,dy) obeys grad f dot tangent=0. Pick three regular points per map, e.g. for f1(2,0),(0,1),(2,1) with tangents(0,1),(1,0),(-2,1); for f2(1,1),(1,2),(2,3) with tangents(1,-1),(1,-2),(2,-3). Origins are a strict maximum (Hessian diag(-1/2,-2)) and a saddle (Hessian [[0,1],[1,0]]). Gradient paths cross regular contours normally, moving toward the hill maximum; the saddle attracts only along one line and repels along another. Optional log r has gradient(x/r²,y/r²), no critical points, and an excluded singular origin.

### c20s2-proj-constrained-design

For open rectangular volume32, area A=xy+2xz+2yz and xyz=32. Eliminate z; stationarity gives x=y=4,z=2,A=48, and reduction in xy plus x+y>=2sqrt(xy) gives the global minimum. Closed cylinder: area2pi r²+2V/r, critical h=2r,r³=V/(2pi), second derivative positive and area blows up at endpoints. Open cylinder: area pi r²+2V/r, h=r,r³=V/pi. Square-section closed tube: area2x²+4V/x, minimum length=x=V^(1/3). Lagrange gives the same relations; positive domains and endpoint behavior verify minima rather than only stationarity.

For line data (0,1),(1,2),(2,2),(3,4),(4,5), residuals mx+b-y give normal equations30m+10b=38,10m+5b=14; m=1,b=4/5. H=2[[30,10],[10,5]] positive definite (det200). H=2X^T X positive by independent columns, since a quadratic zero at five distinct x is zero. ### c20s2-proj-entropy

For S=-sum p_i log p_i, gradient components=-(log p_i+1); Hessian diagonal=-1/p_i. On any nonzero feasible segment its second derivative is negative, so an interior stationary point is the unique global maximum, with continuity 0log0=0 handling boundary comparison as explained locally. Normalization alone gives p_i=1/n, S=log n. For energies(0,1,2) and0<U<2, two independent constraint gradients(1,1,1),(0,1,2) satisfy Ch9 two-constraint hypotheses. Lagrange gives p=C(1,q,q²),q=exp(-beta)>0,C=1/(1+q+q²). U(q)=(q+2q²)/(1+q+q²) has derivative(q²+4q+1)/(1+q+q²)²>0 and limits0,2, so a unique positive q exists by IVT. U=1/2 gives q=(-1+sqrt13)/6 and those probabilities. EndpointU require a point mass, not finite beta.

Support: `c9s5-thm-lagrange-two-constraints` (source/chapters/ch09-gradient-optimization/sections/sec-9-constrained-optimization-and-lagrange-multipliers.xml:364–383); `c20s2-proj-entropy` (source/chapters/ch20-capstone-projects/sections/sec-20-optimization-projects.xml:388–447).

Alpha=.1 gives factors(.9,.6); alpha=.6 gives(.4,-1.4), so bouncing y diverges. For g1 start(1,1),alpha=.2 gives(.6^n,1.4^n), with g_n=.36^n-1.96^n falling while escaping. ### c20s3-proj-mass-from-density

For the supplied equal-area grid, each cell has area4; weighted density sum gives mass60.8 and average60.8/24=38/15. Draw six cell centers with tabulated densities. Each equal absolute measurement error has the same mass weight4; larger density values do not amplify an equal absolute measurement error. Unequal cells require their individual areas. Midpoint versus corner sampling changes sample locations and bias; smooth data usually improves with refinement but noisy/jumping measurements give no guaranteed monotone improvement. Units are density times area (or volume), and the largest density is simply the largest listed cell value.

A complete choice is the uniform supplied rectangular plate, mass4 and Iz=20/3. More generally rectangle sides a,b gives Iz=M(a²+b²)/12,Ix=Mb²/12,Iy=Ma²/12, confirming Iz=Ix+Iy. A disk gives Iz=MR²/2; annulus M(Ro²+Ri²)/2; solid ball 2MR²/5; cylinder about its central axis MR²/2. All follow integrating squared perpendicular distance against density in suitable Cartesian/polar/spherical coordinates. Expanding distance to an axis shifted d gives Icm+Md² because center-of-mass first moments vanish. Moving fixed mass farther out increases I; increased outer density increases the weighted contribution (state whether total mass is held fixed). Units mass times length squared.

Differentiate each supplied map: shear J=1 and area1; diagonal stretch J=ab and area|ab| for nonzero a,b; trapezoid J=1+u with area3/2 and brightness x+y integral2. ### c20s5-project-2-closed-not-exact

Angle omega1=(-y dx+x dy)/(x²+y²) is closed on punctured plane, unit-circle2pi and a circle wholly in x>0 has0 by local potential arctan(y/x). Thus not globally exact; twice winding gives4pi. Radial omega2=(x dx+y dy)/(x²+y²)=d log sqrt(x²+y²) is globally exact on punctured plane and both loop integrals are0: this option correctly distinguishes a hole from an automatic obstruction for every form. Translated angle omega3 repeats the first calculation about its shifted puncture. Choose a half-plane for a legitimate smaller-domain angle; a slit plane gives a continuous branch. Winding and angle questions have a conditional answer for omega2, not a claim that it is also nonexact.

Unit cube radial flux: dPhi=3dV, interior3, positive faces1 each, opposite faces0. Rectangular box[a,b,c] and field(x²,y,z): dPhi=(2x+2)dV, integral bc(a²+2a), positive faces a²bc,abc,abc and three zero faces. Unit-square omega=(x²+y)dx+xy dy: d omega=(y-1)dxdy, integral-1/2, direct edges1/3+1/2-4/3+0=-1/2. Rectangle[-y dx+x dy] yields2ab with compatible orientations. Give all six/four boundary pieces, outward/left conventions, and negate both sides for opposite region orientation.

Support: `c17s2-def-exterior-derivative-plane-1-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-1-form.xml:142–153); `c17s3-def-exterior-derivative-space-2-form` (source/chapters/ch17-exterior-derivatives/sections/sec-17-the-exterior-derivative-of-a-2-form.xml:194–205); `c18s1-def-outward-first-convention` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:435–452); `c18s2-thm-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-theorem.xml:71–88); `c20s5-project-3-generalized-stokes-cube` (source/chapters/ch20-capstone-projects/sections/sec-20-differential-forms-projects.xml:416–515).

Verdict: supported.

### c20s5-project-4-orientation-errors

For annulus inner radius1 outer2 with omega=xdy, correct outer4pi plus inner-pi=3pi; erroneous CCW inner gives5pi. Draw arrows with region on left. For a box face, reversing one normal changes its contribution from f to-f and total by-2f. Swapping surface parameters negates cross product and signed flux; unsigned area stays unchanged. Practical checks: select normal first, induce boundary by right-hand rule, include every hole boundary, and compare sign with physical outward crossing.

Support: `c18s1-def-positive-boundary-orientation-plane` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:213–226); `c18s1-def-boundary-orientation-of-a-surface` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-boundaries-and-orientation.xml:338–352); `c16s6-thm-reparametrize-surface` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-integrating-forms.xml:409–425); `c20s5-project-4-orientation-errors` (source/chapters/ch20-capstone-projects/sections/sec-20-differential-forms-projects.xml:567–681).

Verdict: supported.

### c20s6-proj-quaternions

Use the book quaternion multiplication rules i²=j²=k²=ijk=-1,ij=k,ji=-k, and uv=-u dot v+u cross v. Worked examples(1+i)(1-i)=2 and(i+j)(i-j)=-2k show planar norm/rotation multiplication and noncommutativity. Diagram oriented i,j,k and compare scalar dot versus normal-vector cross versus area-measuring wedge. The requested historical discussion is a writing/research assignment; historical claims require the explicitly requested bibliography, not unannounced mathematical prior knowledge.

Support: `def-c2s2-quaternion` (source/chapters/ch02-vector-products/sections/sec-2-pure-imaginary-quaternions-and-three-dimensional-vectors.xml:38–48); `c2s3-def-dot-cross-from-quaternions` (source/chapters/ch02-vector-products/sections/sec-2-the-scalar-and-vector-parts-of-a.xml:163–175); `c2s7-def-wedge-linear-measurements` (source/chapters/ch02-vector-products/sections/sec-2-alternating-products-a-first-glimpse-of-forms.xml:291–303).

Verdict: supported.

### c20s6-proj-cross-product

Three worked examples: e1 cross e2=e3; e2 cross e1=-e3; (1,0,1)cross(0,1,0)=(-1,0,1), normsqrt2. The three signed projected areas agree with dy dz,dz dx,dx dy evaluations. Diagram the ordered parallelogram. Cross product packages a normal and area efficiently in oriented Euclidean R3 and gives r_u cross r_v for flux; forms make the measuring degree and orientation explicit without confusing vector and area-measurement types.

Support: `c16s3-def-wedge-product-two-covectors` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-the-wedge-product.xml:141–160); `c16s3-ex-tilted-surface-patch` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-the-wedge-product.xml:459–486); `c16s5-def-pullback-coordinate` (source/chapters/ch16-differential-forms-wedge/sections/sec-16-pullbacks.xml:92–107); `c20s6-proj-cross-product` (source/chapters/ch20-capstone-projects/sections/sec-20-writing-projects.xml:160–292).

Verdict: supported.

### c20s6-proj-ftc

Three complete examples: on[1,4],integral d(x²+1)=15=f(4)-f(1); unit-square integral_boundary xdy=1=integral dxdy; unit-cube radial flux3=integral3dV. Include the supplied four-row translation table (FTC, Green, Stokes, divergence), objects and induced boundaries. Signs make internal pieces cancel and retain only boundary data. This satisfies the three-example requirement; a fourth sphere rotation example is optional.

Verdict: supported.

### c20s6-proj-conservation

Choose dye in a fixed unit cube with rho=10-t for0<t<10,J=(x,0,0),s=0. Stored amount10-t has rate-1; right-face outgoing flux1, other faces0. Local rho_t+divJ=-1+1=0. Draw outward right flux, label density/flux/source units. Integrating the local equation and using divergence gives the balance; the reverse every-region argument uses continuity and small neighborhoods. Differentiating a fixed-domain integral invokes the stated19.2 result whose full support has GF-01 extension limitation. Physical sign: outgoing flux subtracts storage; positive s adds production.

Support: `c19s1-def-integral-balance-law` (source/chapters/ch19-conservation-laws/sections/sec-19-balance-laws-from-flux.xml:340–357); `c19s2-thm-local-balance-law` (source/chapters/ch19-conservation-laws/sections/sec-19-the-continuity-equation.xml:197–216); `c18s6-thm-divergence-theorem-as-generalized-stokes` (source/chapters/ch18-generalized-stokes/sections/sec-18-the-divergence-theorem-as-generalized-stokes.xml:212–229); `c20s6-proj-conservation` (source/chapters/ch20-capstone-projects/sections/sec-20-writing-projects.xml:421–587).

Verdict: supported.

## Validation and limits

All assigned XML parsed with lxml. SymPy independently confirmed the quadratic least-squares coefficients `(8/7,1/5,32/35)`, the unequal mixed-partial limits, the positive derivative of the entropy mean-energy function, the rectangle Green integral 6, and the same-boundary counterexample flux pi/2. The source Appendix G midpoint and Monte Carlo function bodies were also executed: constant rectangular quadrature returned24 and an empty Monte Carlo mask returned0; two reproducible pond sampling runs are recorded in the Monte Carlo witness. No rendered-page inspection, graphical production, software installation, general API compatibility test, probabilistic convergence proof, or full textbook correctness certification is claimed. All diagram geometry and labels were read in source.
